We can also use "peak shaving" coordinate operation: Identify top coefficient k*. It is sum over i=0..min(n-1,k) a_i a_{k-i}. To reduce it, we can transfer mass from positions i in this anti-diagonal to positions not contributing to top peaks. This is a direct heuristic:
- For top few peaks K, define penalty for each a_j = sum over peaks k of a_{k-j} (derivative). Move mass from high derivative positions to low derivative positions while preserving sum/nonneg. This is same gradient.
But can do coordinate pair swaps: choose j_high with large derivative, j_low small, move small mass δ from high to low. Use line search. This may improve last bits efficiently. We can implement a "pair move" ILS:

   - For random pair (high, low), move δ = α * p[high] maybe, p[high]-=δ, p[low]+=δ, normalize; evaluate. Accept if improves.
This is like simplex conditional probability distribution optimizing. It can escape rounding issues. But pairwise random O(n) selection. Could be many.

   - Run active polish for e.g., 20s.
   - Occasionally (every 60s) generate a random perturbation and quick optimize for 10-20s; accept if improves.
This can be called with various budgets.

def _score_simple(a):
    n=len(a)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-15: return inf
    c=_full_convolve(a,a)
    m=c.max()
    return 2.0*n*m/(s*s)
```

def _next_pow2(n):

Now smooth objective and gradient:
```
def _smooth_obj(z,beta,n):
    a=_softmax(z)
    c=_self_conv(a)
    m=c.max()
    shifted=(c-m)/beta
    e=np.exp(np.clip(shifted,-700,0))
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return 2*n*m
    return 2*n*(m + beta*np.log(s))
def _smooth_grad(z,beta,n):
    a=_softmax(z)
    c=_self_conv(a); m=c.max()
    shifted=(c-m)/beta; e=np.exp(np.clip(shifted,-700,0)); s=e.sum()
    if s<1e-300: w=zeros; w[argmax]=1
    else: w=e/s
    h=_corr_hp(a,w)  # length n
    Cw=float(np.dot(w,c))
    grad=4.0*n*a*(h-Cw*a)
    return grad
```
Note Cw = m+beta log s, also weighted avg c. Sum of grad should be 0: ∑a h = Cw? ∑ a_j h_j = ∑_k w_k ∑_j a_j a_{k-j}=∑_k w_k c_k = Cw. Thus grad sum =4n(∑a h - Cw ∑a^2?) Wait ∑ a_j (h_j - Cw a_j)=∑a h - Cw∑a^2. ∑a^2 not ∑a=1. Oops my earlier chain formula may be wrong! Let's derive carefully.

For p = softmax(z), ∂p_i/∂z_j = p_j(δ_{ij}-p_i). Objective F=2n C_smooth. ∂F/∂p_i = 4n h_i? where h_i=∑_k w_k p_{k-i} (since ∂c_k/∂p_i=2 w? actually C_smooth = ∑ w_k c_k; derivative =2∑w p_{k-i}; times 2n =4n h_i). Then gradient wrt z_j = ∑_i p_j(δ_{ij}-p_i) 4n h_i = 4n p_j h_j -4n p_j ∑_i p_i h_i. ∑_i p_i h_i = ∑_k w_k ∑_i p_i p_{k-i} = ∑ w_k c_k = Cw. So grad_z=4n p (h - Cw p). I used a*(h-Cw*a), where * is elementwise, not a*sum? `Cw*a` elementwise. Sum of grad =4n(∑p h - Cw ∑p^2). Not necessarily zero because softmax gradient lies in tangent space sum0? Wait ∑_j ∂p_j/∂z_j =0 for each j? The derivative matrix M = P - pp^T has row/col sums 0: ∑_j M_{j,i}=0 and ∑_i M_{i,j}=0. When taking gradient vector d_i = ∑_j F_j M_{j,i}? Need indexing. If F vector wrt p, grad_z_i = ∑_p ∂F/∂p_p ∂p_p/∂z_i = ∑_p F_p p_i(δ_{pi}-p_p)=p_i F_i - p_i ∑_p F_p p_p. So grad = p∘F - p*(p^T F). Sum grad =1^T p∘F - (p^T F)(1^T p)=p^T F - p^T F=0. In our expression, F_i=4n h_i, p^T F=4n Cw. So grad=4n(p∘h - p*(p^T h)) =4n p(h - Cw p). Sum=∑p h - Cw∑p^2? Wait ∑p*(Cw p) = Cw ∑p_i^2, not Cw*1. But p^T F uses F_p p_p, sum p_i F_i. In grad sum, ∑ [p_i F_i - p_i ∑p_p F_p] = p^T F - (p^T F)∑p_i =0. Our expression term is 4n p_i (h_i - Cw p_i). Sum =4n(∑p_i h_i - Cw ∑p_i^2). But Cw = ∑w c = ∑? Is ∑p_i h_i = Cw? h_i=∑k w_k p_{k-i}; ∑p_i h_i=∑w_k ∑p_i p_{k-i}=∑w_k c_k =Cw. Then sum =4n(Cw - Cw ∑p_i^2) not zero unless ∑p_i^2=1. Contradiction indicates F_i not 4n h_i? Let's check chain derivative of softmax: p_j wrt z_i = p_i(δ_{ij}-p_j). Then grad_z_i = ∑_j ∂F/∂p_j * p_i(δ_{ji}-p_j) = p_i F_i - p_i ∑_j p_j F_j. That's p_i F_i - p_i (p^T F). This is p∘F - p*(p^T F). If F=4n h, p^T F=4n Cw. So grad=4n(p h_i - p_i Cw) =same. Sum = ∑p F_i - ∑p_i^2 Cw =4nCw -4nCw∑p_i^2? But first term ∑p F_i =4n∑p_i h_i =4nCw. Second ∑p_i (Cw p_i)=Cw∑p_i^2. Missing factor: In expression p_i * (p^T F) = p_i * (4n Cw). Sum = (sum p_i) 4n Cw =4nCw. It should be p_i * scalar, not p_i*Cw*p_i? Wait p^T F =4n Cw. Then grad_i= p_i F_i - p_i * (4n Cw) =4n p_i h_i -4n Cw p_i. That is 4n p_i (h_i - Cw), NOT h_i - Cw p_i. I erroneously multiplied p_i again. Let's recalc: p^T F = ∑_j p_j F_j. Since F_j scalar. So grad_i = p_i F_i - p_i (p^T F). If F_i=4n h_i, p^T F=4nCw. So grad=4n p_i (h_i - Cw). This sum zero. Good. The previous program used `4.0 * n * a * (h - W)` where W=dot(w,c)=Cw. They had h-W, not h-W*a. I initially miscopied. Good. Use `_smograd` as previous: `return 4.0 * n * a * (h - W)`. This has gradient of objective with p sum1. Great.

Objective F uses 2n C_smooth, gradient matches.

Now active true max gradient: Use w uniform over active (sum1), h, W = dot(w,c). Objective subgradient F=2n C_max; derivative weight sum1? If F= max c *2n, subgradient w sum1 over active, ∂F/∂p=2n? Wait max F=2n M; subgradient wrt p: 2n∑_{active uniform} ∂c_k = 2n*2? For each active c derivative 2p_{k-i}; with weights sum1 => 2n*2 h =4n h. p^T F=4n W (where W=average active c not M because weights sum1 and c active maybe all M, if uniform active all equal M so W=M). If active all exact M, W=M. Then grad_z=4n p(h - W). Same formula. Good.

So `_grad_weights(z or a, w)` returns 4n a (h - dot(w,c)). We can compute objective separately.

This gradient is cheaper.

Now L-BFGS problem class with current beta using global variables? Let's implement class:
```
class _SmoothProblem:
    def __init__(self,beta,n): self.beta=beta; self.n=n
    def f(self,z): return _smooth_obj(z,self.beta,self.n)
    def g(self,z): return _smooth_grad(z,self.beta,self.n)
```
Scipy minimize accepts prob.f as function. Good.

def _lbfgs_anneal(a_init, deadline, min_remaining=30, beta_start=None, extra_betas=None):
    n=len(a_init); a=_normalize(a_init); z=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)); z-=z.mean()

    c=_self_conv(a); M=c.max()
    if beta_start is None:
       # gap top 50

       gap=top[0]-top[-1]
       beta_start=max(M*0.01, gap*0.5, 1e-18)
    betas=np.array([1.0,0.6,0.4,0.25,0.15,0.1,0.06,0.04,0.025,0.015,0.01,0.006,0.004,0.002,0.001,0.0006,0.0003,0.00015,0.00008,0.00004,0.00002,0.00001,0.000005,0.000002,0.000001,0.0000005]) * beta_start
    min_beta=max(M*1e-9, 1e-18)
    betas=np.array([b for b in betas if b>min_beta]); if empty...
    current_z=z

       if time + min_remaining > deadline: break

Detailed `_active_polish`:
```
def _active_polish(a_init, deadline, min_remaining=10, beta_factor_start=1e-3, beta_factor_end=1e-6):
    n=len(a_init); a=_normalize(a_init.copy()); best_a=a; best_sc=_score(a)
    z=np.log(a+1e-300); z-=z.mean()
    beta_factor=beta_factor_start
    lr=0.5  # maybe
    momentum=0.85
    v=np.zeros(n)
    stall=0
    while now + min_remaining < deadline:
        a=softmax(z); sc=_score(a); c=_self_conv(a); M=c.max()
        if sc<best... 
        # choose w
        shifted=(c-M)/(M*beta_factor+1e-30)
        e=np.exp(np.clip(shifted,-700,0)); s=e.sum()
        if s<...: w uniform over argmax where c>=M-M*1e-8
        else w=e/s
        h=_corr_hp(a,w); W=dot(w,c); grad=4*n*a*(h-W)
        gnorm=norm(grad)
        if gnorm<1e-18: break
        v = momentum*v - lr*grad
        # direction = -v? If v is heavy ball negative? Let's define descent direction d = v (including -lr grad). 
        # heavy ball: v = momentum*v - lr*grad; new z = z + v? Since grad positive to increase, v negative. line search alpha around 1: z_try=z+alpha*v.
        best_alpha=0; best_score=sc; best_z=z
        for alpha in [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 2.0, 0.0625]:
             if alpha<=0: continue
             z_try=z+alpha*v
             a_try=softmax(z_try); sc_try=_score(a_try)
             if sc_try<best_score: best_score=sc_try; best_alpha=alpha; best_z=z_try
        if best_alpha>0:
             z=best_z; stall=0
             # adapt lr: if best_alpha==1 and best_score<sc-... increase lr *=1.05 maybe capped; if alpha small reduce.
             if best_alpha >=1.0: lr=min(5.0,lr*1.05)
             else: lr=max(1e-8,lr*best_alpha)
             # update beta_factor slowly down
             beta_factor=max(beta_factor_end,beta_factor*0.995)
        else:
             v=np.zeros(n); stall+=1
             lr=max(1e-12,lr*0.5)
             if stall%20==0: beta_factor=max(beta_factor_end,beta_factor*0.5)
             if stall>100: z=best_z_best; v=0; lr=0.5; beta_factor=1e-4; stall=0
    return best_a,best_sc
```
Need ensure `v` magnitude not too large. `grad` components may be O(1?), lr 0.5, v maybe O(1). z change O(1) could be huge. Maybe lr should be much smaller. Let's estimate grad magnitude for near optimum. c max ~3e-5, h~c, difference maybe M*1e-5=3e-10? For weights close to max, h-W maybe small. 4n=131072, a=3e-5, product ~131072*3e-5*3e-10=1.2e-9? Hmm tiny. If difference 1e-6, grad~0.004. lr 0.5 gives z change 0.002, okay. If grad larger early, line search reduces. Starting lr=1 maybe ok. The line search includes alpha 2, but if v large, alpha small. We can scale direction by gnorm: set d = v/(gnorm+eps) and use step length in z units, e.g., step=0.1? Then line search step_size. Simpler: after computing grad, normalize direction? But gradient curvature scale matters.

d = -grad

for step in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0]: z_try=z+step*d*??
```

Let's think about possible **exact active-set linear system** to jump to KKT. We can implement a simple Newton on active constraints:
Given p, M, active set A where c≈M. We want find d (sum0) such that c_k(p+d)=M - δ for k∈A? Linearize: c_k + 2∑_i p_i? Wait derivative wrt p_j =2∑_{k}w? For single active k, linear change Δc_k ≈2p_k? 2∑_i? Actually derivative wrt p_j=2p_{k-j} (ordered) for c_k. For a set with weights, linear. We can choose d to reduce active c as much as possible subject to ||d||^2≤ρ, sum d=0. This is min μ s.t. G_A d ≤ -μ, ||d||≤ρ. We can solve by projected gradient but maybe use closed form if constraints many. Not needed.

What about **using convex optimization of weights w for active set** to satisfy KKT: For current p and active set A, find w≥0 sum1 that minimizes ||H_w p - mean||^2. This is a convex QP. We can approximately solve with gradient:
- B(p) is matrix n×|A| with B_{j,k}=2p_{k-j} valid. Need w such that B w ≈ average. Since B can be applied via FFT (h = B w/2?) Actually h for weights w (sum maybe) = ∑ w_k p_{k-j}; H_w p/2 =? Our h. We want h_j ≈ constant. This is a least squares problem in w with linear operator B. We can use conjugate gradient/FISTA, but B^T? B^T h? Could compute via convolution too. But maybe too much. A simple coordinate update can find w: start w uniform active; while iterations, w_j proportional to exp(-η (B w - mean_j)^2)? Since minimize ∑(Bw-mean)^2. Gradient wrt w_k =2(Bw-mean)_j B_{j,k}. Could apply. Then w normalized. Then gradient direction from h. This adaptively finds weights that equalize gradient, potentially better than exp(c) weights. We can use this as an advanced active polish.

Algorithm `find_weights(a, active_mask, iters)`:
- w uniform on active.
- for it:
   h = ∑w p_{k-j}.
   target mean = mean(h) (since sum p=1 and ∑w=1? h mean? ∑a h? Not mean h. We want H_w a constant, i.e., h_j constant because gradient condition H_w a=2M1? h_j = (H_w a)_j/2? H_w a_j=2∑w_k p_{k-j}=2h_j. So want h_j constant. target=mean(h).
   r = h - target.
   grad_w_k = 2∑_j r_j p_{k-j}? Because ∂h_j/∂w_k=p_{k-j}. To minimize 1/2∑r^2, gradient w = B^T r. We need compute B^T r: for each k, ∑_j r_j p_{k-j}. This is correlation of r and p, similar `_corr_hp(p, r)`? It gives y_j=∑_i p_i r_{i+j}. If set r as weights? Let q = _corr_hp(p, r) length n? For k up to2n-2, need length 2n-1. We only have n? `_corr_hp(p,w)`:
```
def _corr_hp(p,w):
    n=len(p)
    N=_next_pow2(2*n)
    # w length 2n-1, reverse
    wr = w[::-1]
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    W=np.fft.rfft(wr,N)  # wr length <N
    c=np.fft.irfft(P*W,N)
    # extract t=2n-2-j
    idx = np.arange(n)
    return c[2*n-2 - idx]
```
Could avoid allocation idx by slicing: `c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]` because indices from 2n-2 down to n-1. Yes `c[n-1:2*n-1]` gives [c[n-1],...,c[2n-2]], reverse. `return c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]`. Good.
But if N=next_pow2(2n), for n=32768 N=65536, c length N. indices up to 65534 okay. For n=1, 2n-2=0.

Need ensure no alias for gradient with N=2n? Let's double-check for n not power. N=next_pow2(2n). Let L=2n-1, max linear index 3n-3. For t in [n-1,2n-2]. Aliasing s=t+N. Minimal s=(n-1)+N. Need >3n-3 => N >2n-2. Since N≥2n (next pow2 of 2n) and 2n >2n-2, no alias. Good. No other s=t-qN negative. Great.

What about using N=next_pow2(2n) for self conv? Linear length 2n-1, N≥2n => no alias. Good.

We can define `_fft_len(n): return 1 << (max(1,2*n)-1).bit_length()`? Need next power of two ≥2n. For 2n=65536, (65535).bit_length=16, 1<<16=65536. For 2n=49152, (49151).bit_length=16? 49151 binary <65536, bit_length=16, =>65536. Good. If 2n=1? n=1,2 => next1? (1).bit_length=1, 1<<0? Formula `1 << (x-1).bit_length()`: for x=2, (1).bit_length=1 ->2; for x=1, (0).bit_length=0 ->1. So:
```
def _next_pow2_ge(x):
    x=max(1,int(x))
    return 1 << (x-1).bit_length()
```

Now `_softmax`:
```
def _softmax(z):
    z=np.asarray(z,dtype=np.float64)
    z=z-z.max()
    # If max nan? fallback
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)): return np.ones_like(z)/len(z)
    e=np.exp(np.clip(z,-700.0,0.0))
    s=e.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: return uniform
    return e/s
```
Since z subtract max, max=0, min could -inf; clip.

`_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(a):
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if not finite or s<1e-300: return np.ones_like(a)/len(a)
    return a/s
```

```
def _resample(a,new_n):
   old_n=len(a)
   if new_n==old_n: return a.copy()
   if old_n==1: return np.full(new_n,a[0]/new_n)
   # Use x in [0,1) maybe? For interpolation at integers? To preserve endpoint alignment. Use np.linspace(0,1,old_n) and new. This maps endpoints. Could also use (i+0.5)/n to avoid endpoints? Previous used 0,1. Fine.
   xo=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,old_n)
   xn=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n)
   b=np.interp(xn,xo,a)
   return _normalize(b)
```
Maybe linear interpolation of a normalized sum doesn't preserve sum exactly but normalize. For large changes, maybe use cubic? scipy.interpolate available? Could use `np.interp` only. Good.

def _chunk_maxit(n, remaining):

Now active polish line search. Let's implement robustly with normalized direction and step. Use `score` each candidate. To speed, line search candidates maybe 5. For n=32768, 5 conv per iter. If 0.002s each, 0.01s; 50k iter possible. Good.

```
def _active_polish(a_init, deadline, min_remaining=5, beta_factor_start=1e-4, beta_factor_end=1e-7, init_step=0.05):
    a=_normalize(a_init.copy()); best_a=a; best_sc=_score(a)
    z=np.log(a+1e-300); z-=z.mean()
    best_z=z.copy()
    vel=np.zeros(n)
    step=init_step
    beta_factor=beta_factor_start
    momentum=0.9
    stall=0; no_best=0
    while time + min_rem < deadline:
        if not finite z: break
        a=softmax(z); c=_self_conv(a); M=c.max(); sc=2nM (sum1)
        if sc < best_sc - 1e-14: update
        # if M extremely small? no
        denom = M * beta_factor
        if denom < 1e-300: denom=1e-300
        shifted=(c-M)/denom
        e=np.exp(np.clip(shifted,-700,0)); s=e.sum()
        if s < 1e-12:
            w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[np.argmax(c)] = 1.0
        else: w=e/s
        h=_corr_hp(a,w); W=float(np.dot(w,c)); grad=4.0*n*a*(h-W)
        # ensure finite
        if not finite grad: break
        gmax=np.max(np.abs(grad))
        if gmax < 1e-18:
             # try uniform z? break
             break
        direction = -grad / gmax
        direction -= direction.mean()
        vel = momentum*vel + direction
        vnorm=np.max(np.abs(vel))
        if vnorm < 1e-18 or not finite: vel=direction; vnorm=...
        vdir = vel / vnorm
        # line search steps
        best_local_sc=sc; best_local_z=z; best_step_used=step
        steps = [step, step*0.5, step*2.0, step*0.25, step*4.0, step*0.125]
        # Also try steepest without momentum:
        for alpha_mult in steps:
            zt = z + alpha_mult*vdir
            at=softmax(zt); ct=_self_conv(at); Mt=ct.max(); sc_t=2nMt
            if sc_t < best_local_sc - 1e-15:
                best_local_sc=sc_t; best_local_z=zt; best_step_used=alpha_mult
        # Also try mean-zero reversal? maybe not
        if best_local_z is not z:
            z=best_local_z; a=softmax(z); sc=best_local_sc; update best; stall=0
            # adapt step: if best_step_used >= step*2? increase; if <=0.25 step decrease
            if best_step_used > step * 1.5: step=min(0.5, step*1.2)
            elif best_step_used < step * 0.5: step=max(1e-5, step*0.6)
            beta_factor=max(beta_factor_end, beta_factor*0.99)
        else:
            stall +=1
            vel=np.zeros(n)
            if stall % 10 ==0:
                step=max(1e-5, step*0.5)
                beta_factor=max(beta_factor_end, beta_factor*0.5)
            if stall > 80:
                # restore best and try larger step/beta
                z=best_z.copy(); step=init_step; beta_factor=1e-5; vel=zeros; stall=0
                # maybe add tiny random perturbation to escape
                z += 0.001*np.random.standard_normal(n)?? Need rng; can use np.random.
                z -= z.mean()
        # symmetrize occasionally?
        if (it%50==0):
            a2=a+a[::-1]; a2=normalize(a2); sc2=score(a2); if sc2<sc: update z=... 
```
Need item counter.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget_s-20
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # load parent
    ...
    best_seq,best_score = parent...

    # If parent exists, evaluate resamples for candidate lengths and select top.
    cand_lengths=[]

    if budget_s > 1200: lengths=[24576,28000,30000,31000,32000,32768,40000,48000,49152,65536]
    elif budget_s >700: lengths=[24576,30000,32000,32768,49152]
    else: lengths=[24576,32768]
    quick=[]
    for nn in lengths:
       if nn<=0 or nn>100000: continue
       a0=_resample(parent,nn) if parent else _make_profile(nn,'uniform')
       sc0=_score(a0)
       quick.append((sc0,nn,a0))
    quick.sort()
    # Choose main list: first, second, and one larger if time. Ensure not duplicate.
    selected=[]
    for sc,nn,a0 in quick:
       if len(selected)<2 and nn not in [x[1] for x in selected]: selected.append((nn,a0,sc))
    # If budget enough, add 49152 or max nn if not selected
    if budget_s > 1000:
       for sc,nn,a0 in quick:
          if nn>=40000 and nn not in selected lengths and len(selected)<3:
             selected.append(...); break
    # If selected empty use 32768
```

Pseudo:
```
def _local_optimize(a_init, deadline, min_total=30):
    n=len(a_init)
    a=_normalize(a_init.copy())
    best_a=a; best_sc=_score(a)
    # Phase 1: L-BFGS for 55% of budget
    budget=deadline-now
    if budget > 30:
       lb_deadline = now + 0.55*budget
       a,sc=_lbfgs_anneal(a, lb_deadline, min_remaining=10)
       if sc<best: update
    # Phase 2: active for 15%
    if now + 10 < deadline:
       pol_deadline = min(deadline, now + 0.15*budget)
       a,sc=_active_polish(a, pol_deadline, min_remaining=5)
       update
    # Phase 3: ILS quick for rest
    if now + 20 < deadline:
       ils_deadline = deadline - 5
       best_a,best_sc = _ils(best_a, ils_deadline, rng?)  # Need rng global? Create inside with seed based on len?
    return best_a,best_sc
```

ILS function:
```
def _ils(a_init, deadline, rng, min_time=30, quick_budget=15):
   current=_normalize(a_init); current_sc=_score(current)
   best=current; best_sc=current_sc
   while now + quick_budget + 5 < deadline:
      kind=int(rng.integers(0,5))
      a_pert = _perturb(current, kind, rng)
      # quick local: active polish for up to quick_budget, or small L-BFGS if quick_budget>40
      if quick_budget > 40:
          a_opt,sc = _quick_local(a_pert, min(15, quick_budget), rng?)
      else:
          a_opt,sc = _active_polish(a_pert, now+quick_budget, min_remaining=2)
      if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
          best=a_opt; best_sc=sc; current=a_opt
      elif sc < current_sc - 1e-7:
          current=a_opt
      # if repeated no improvement, increase perturbation? simple
   return best,best_sc
```

def _entropy_direction(a, M, deadline, max_iter=2000):
   n=len(a)
   y=np.ones(2*n-1)
   last_phi=np.inf
   a_pos=np.maximum(a,1e-300)
   for it in range(max_iter):
      h = _corr_hp(a_pos, y)
      h = np.maximum(h, 1e-300)
      g = 1.0/h
      # scale g? For direction only, scale irrelevant; but v=A g depends on scale. If h=1, g=1; okay.
      v = _conv(a_pos, g)
      # ensure real
      delta = v - M
      # update direction
      scale = max(np.max(np.abs(delta)), 1e-300)
      d = delta / scale
      # try alphas
      best_phi=inf; best_y=None
      for alpha in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,2.0,0.05]:
          y_try = y + alpha*d
          if (y_try<0).any(): ... project? Use np.maximum(y_try,0)
          y_try = np.maximum(y_try, 1e-300) # maybe project
          # compute phi
          h_try = _corr_hp(a_pos, y_try)
          h_try=np.maximum(h_try,1e-300)
          # g_try=1/h_try; v_try=conv(a,g_try) not needed for phi
          phi = -np.sum(np.log(h_try)) + M*np.sum(y_try)
          if finite and phi<best_phi: best...
      if best_y is None or best_phi > last_phi: break?

      # Check convergence: max |delta| small? or g shape similar.
      if np.max(np.abs(delta)) < 0.01*max(1,M?)? maybe break after many.
   g=1/h; return _normalize(g)
```

Let's implement `_entropy_direction` robustly:
```
def _entropy_direction(a, max_iters=300, time_budget=5.0, eta=0.5):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-300)
    log_y=np.zeros(L)
    eta=eta
    last_std=2.0
    g_out=None
    t0=time.monotonic()
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_budget: break
        # normalize log_y
        log_y = log_y - np.max(log_y)
        y=np.exp(log_y)
        s=y.sum()
        if not finite or s<=0:
            y=np.ones(L); s=L
        y = y/s * L
        h=_corr_hp(a,y)
        h=np.maximum(h,1e-300)
        g=1.0/h
        v=_conv(a,g)  # length L
        v=np.maximum(v,0)
        v_max=float(v.max())
        if not finite or v_max<=0: break
        r = v / v_max
        mean_r=float(r.mean())
        # We only care about reducing peaks; use positive part? If r-mean, high positive, low negative. Could be too much low negative, zeroing y. Use asymmetric: increase high, mildly decrease low:
        r_centered = r - mean_r
        # Optional clip to [-0.5*maxpos, maxpos]?
        update = r_centered
        # To avoid killing y where v low, use `update = np.where(r_centered>0, r_centered, 0.5*r_centered)`
        log_y += eta * update
        # adapt eta based on change? optional
        # convergence check every 20
        it%20==0:
            std=float(np.std(r))
            if abs(std-last_std)<1e-6 and std<0.05: break
            last_std=std
            if std > last_std*1.5: eta*=0.7
            elif std < last_std*0.8: eta=min(1.0, eta*1.05)
    # after loop, ensure y
    log_y = log_y - np.max(log_y); y=np.exp(log_y); y/=y.sum()*L?
    h=_corr_hp(a,y); g=1/h; return _normalize(g)
```
The update may make y positive all. If y becomes very concentrated, h maybe small for some j, g huge. Mixing with such g may be bad but line search safe.

Inner time budget: If 5s and 300 iters, okay.

Apply direction line search:
```
def _mix_improve(a, g):
   current=a; sc=_score(a)
   best=a; best_sc=sc
   for alpha in [0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0]:
       m=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*g
       m=_normalize(m)
       sc_m=_score(m)
       if sc_m < best_sc - 1e-14: best=m; best_sc=sc_m
   return best,best_sc
```
Maybe alpha>1 extrapolate? If g much better, could try 1.2? But mixing only convex combination; if g itself better, alpha=1. If direction as gradient, maybe extrapolate in p-space: a + α(g-a). We can include alphas >1, up to 1.5, and normalize/clipping. But nonnegativity; if negative, clip. Could help. Use:
```
for alpha in [0.1,...,1.0,1.2,1.5]: m=a+alpha*(g-a); if min>=-1e-9 normalize else clip.
```
Safe accept.

Let's include entropy direction phase:
```
def _entropy_phase(a, deadline, min_remaining=20):
   best=a; best_sc=score
   while now+min_remaining<deadline:
      g=_entropy_direction(best, max_iters=300, time_budget=min(5, ...), eta=0.5)
      a2,sc2=_mix_improve(best,g)
      if sc2<best_sc-1e-12: best=a2; best_sc=sc2
      else:
          # perturb g or a slightly and retry? or break if no improve for 3
      # Also try g with current a reversed? maybe
   return best,best_sc
```
This may be slower if no improve. We can limit.

Maybe the state-of-art LP direction maximizes sum g under a*g≤M; our entropy direction may not maximize sum but shape. We can further improve by maximizing sum of g while keeping a*g feasible? The entropy objective scales; with y normalized, we lose scale. To get max sum, after shape g_shape, we can scale it so max(a*g)=M: `g_feas = g_shape * (M / max(a*g_shape))`. The sum is then S = (M/vmax)*sum(g_shape). If S≥1? Since a itself feasible with sum1 (max a*a=M, a*a? Wait a*g with g=a: v=a*a≤M; max=M; g=a feasible sum1). Our direction normalized sum1 may have v_max >? If we scale to max M, sum may be >1. We can choose scale = min? For mixing, we need normalized g; scale irrelevant. But sum of feasible scaled version indicates direction quality. We can report.

Maybe use scale to mix in original scale: Let g_feas = g_shape * (M / v_max). This g_feas satisfies a*g≤M? If v conv with g_feas max M yes. Sum maybe S_g. State-of-art rescale g to normalization (sum√(2n)). For normalized sum1, g_norm = g_feas/S_g = g_shape/sum(g_shape). Same. Good.

Could compute S_g; if S_g < 1, direction not better than a? But a itself has sum1 feasible. The max-entropy optimum should S_g≥1. If S_g<1, discard. Nice filter.

Now, can we solve the **max sum LP primal** approximately using the same y? The max-entropy point may have lower sum than LP optimum, but likely decent.

Another potential formula: For fixed a, if we want maximize sum g subject to a*g≤M, g is the solution to dual covering. The entropy point might be interior. Good enough.

Let's think about final improvement: Perhaps the best way to beat target is to run entropy direction loop for a long time on n=32768, as it directly enforces a*g flatness and may approach KKT better than gradient. We can design local optimize to use it heavily:
- Start a (parent resample).
- While time:
   * Run `_entropy_hill` for e.g., 60s (updates a if improves).
   * Run active polish for 20s.
   * Run small L-BFGS for 30s to change basin.
This uses different descent info.

Need avoid too much code complexity causing bugs. We can write straightforward.

`_conv(a,b)`:
```
def _conv(a,b):
    n=len(a); m=len(b)
    out=n+m-1
    N=_next_pow2_ge(max(out, n+m-1?))  # for general, need N>=out to avoid full alias. Use out.
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)
    return c[:out]
```
For `_self_conv`, can call `_conv(a,a)` but F computed twice; optimize:
```
def _self_conv(a):
   n=len(a); out=2*n-1; N=_next_pow2_ge(out)
   F=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   return np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:out]
```
For `_entropy_direction`, `v=_conv(a,g)` where both len n, out=2n-1, N as above. Good.

`_corr_hp(p,w)` uses N=_next_pow2_ge(2*n) but out linear 3n-2; for coefficients we want, N≥2n is enough as proven. But if n=1? L=1, t=0, c[0]? `c[n-1:2*n-1]` = c[0:1]? Need slice `c[0:1]` since 2n-1=1 exclusive? In Python c[n-1:2*n-1] gives 0:1? n=1 => [0:1] yes includes c[0]. Reverse okay. For n=1, p length1, w length1, wr length1, conv circular N=2. c[0]=p0 wr0? h0=p0 w0. Good.

Potential issue in `_corr_hp`: `w` length L=2n-1 may be >N? For N=next_pow2_ge(2n), L=2n-1 ≤N. Good.

```
def _corr_hp(p,w):
   n=len(p)
   N=_next_pow2_ge(2*n)
   wr = w[::-1]
   P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   W=np.fft.rfft(wr,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(P*W,N)
   return c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1].copy()
```
Need ensure `w` is contiguous? okay.

`_score(a)`:
```
def _score(a):
   a=np.asarray(a)
   n=len(a); s=float(a.sum())
   if s <= 1e-15 or not finite: return inf
   c=_self_conv(a)
   m=float(c.max())
   if not finite: return inf
   return float(2.0*n*m/(s*s))
```
In optimization, a normalized sum1, can use `_score1(a)` no sum. But keep.

def _weights_from_c(c,beta):

   if s<1e-300: w=zeros; w[argmax]=1 else w=e/s; return w

`_lbfgs_anneal` details:
```
def _lbfgs_anneal(a, deadline, min_remaining=15, beta_start_factor=0.02):
    n=len(a)
    a=_normalize(a.copy())
    best_a=a; best_sc=_score(a)
    z=np.log(a+1e-300); z=z-z.mean(); best_z=z.copy()
    # factors list
    factors = [...]
    for fac in factors:
       if time.monotonic()+min_remaining >= deadline: break
       c=_self_conv(a); M=float(c.max())
       if M<=0: break
       beta=max(M*fac, M*1e-12)
       if beta <= 0: break
       prob=_SmoothProblem(beta,n)
       # Run chunks from current z
       while time.monotonic()+min_remaining < deadline:
           maxit = _chunk_maxit(n, deadline, min_remaining)
           try:
              res=minimize(prob.f, z, jac=prob.g, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':maxit, 'ftol':1e-18, 'gtol':1e-14, 'maxcor':50, 'maxls':50})
           except Exception:
              break
           if not np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)): break
           sc_new=_score(_softmax(res.x))
           if sc_new < best_sc - 1e-15:
               best_a=_softmax(res.x); best_sc=sc_new; best_z=res.x.copy()
           # decide update z
           if res.fun < 1e300:
               # If fun improved from previous? We need previous fun start? Store fun for current z.
               new_fun=res.fun
               # Since prob.f may be called at changed z; we can compute old_fun? We'll store `current_fun=prob.f(z)` before; but expensive. We can accept if new_fun < global? Maybe if sc_new <= best_sc + 1e-6? 
               # To avoid moving to worse, update z if sc_new <= _score(a) + 1e-6? But a_current may not be best. Let's update z to res.x if (sc_new < _score(a) + 1e-5) or res.fun lower estimated.
           # set a = softmax(res.x); z=res.x if finite
           z=res.x; a=_softmax(z)
           if res.status == 0 or res.success: break
           # if runtime low break
       # after beta, maybe set a=best_a? if current worse.
       if best_sc < _score(a) - 1e-7:
           a=best_a.copy()
           z=np.log(a+1e-300); z-=z.mean()
    return best_a,best_sc
```
Should we always update z to res.x? If smooth descent moved to a point with worse true score but lower smooth for current beta, next smaller beta may recover. But if it worsens a lot, could waste. We can maintain `a_current` and `best_a`. At start of each beta, use `a_current`. If after beta `a_current` score worse than best by >1e-4, reset to best? Maybe. Let's do:
- `current_a` initially a.
- For beta chunks, start z from current_a.
- After each chunk, if `res.status` maxiter and time, continue. If `res.fun` not finite break.
- We set `current_a=softmax(res.x)` unconditionally if score increase < 0.02? If huge, keep previous z? We can check `sc_cur = _score(current_a)`. If `sc_new < sc_cur + 1e-4`, update current; else revert and maybe break this beta.
This balances.

`_chunk_maxit` using remaining:
```
def _chunk_maxit(n, deadline, min_rem):
    rem=max(1.0, deadline-min_rem-time.monotonic())
    if rem < 10: return 20
    if n <= 1024: return 3000
    if n <= 2048: return 2000
    if n <= 4096: return 1000
    if n <= 8192: return 500
    if n <= 16384: return 250
    if n <= 32768: return 120
    if n <= 65536: return 60
    return 30
```
This is conservative; L-BFGS may converge slowly but many chunks. For n=32768, 120 iters per chunk. If 0.05s/iter chunk6s; if 0.2s chunk24s. Okay.

Need set `maxls=20` maybe.

`_entropy_hill`:
```
def _entropy_hill(a, deadline, min_remaining=10, rng=None):
   best=_normalize(a); best_sc=score
   stall=0
   while now+min_rem<deadline:
      inner_time=min(8.0, deadline-min_rem-now)
      if inner_time<2: break
      g=_entropy_direction(best, max_iters=400, time_budget=inner_time*0.8, eta=0.5)
      if not finite g: break
      a2,sc2=_mix_improve(best,g)
      if sc2 < best_sc - 1e-12:
         best=a2; best_sc=sc2; stall=0
      else:
         stall+=1
         if stall>5:
             # try perturb best slightly and direction
             bestp=_perturb(best,0,rng,0.05) if rng else best
             g2=_entropy_direction(bestp, max_iters=200,time_budget=inner_time*0.5)
             a3,sc3=_mix_improve(bestp,g2)
             if sc3<best_sc-1e-12: best=a3; best_sc=sc3; stall=0
             else: break
   return best,best_sc
```
But if g equals best, no improvement. Inner 5s each, 180 iterations maybe. Good.

def _quick_local(a, deadline, rng=None):

   return best,best_sc

Perturb strategies:
```
def _perturb(a, kind, rng, strength=None):
   n=len(a); z=np.log(a+1e-300); z-=z.mean()
   if kind==0: # global small
       s = 0.05 if strength is None else strength
       z += s*rng.standard_normal(n)
   elif kind==1: # global medium
       s = 0.15
       z += s*rng.standard_normal(n)
   elif kind==2: # block
       block=max(50,n//10); start=rng.integers(0,n-block); z[start:start+block] += 0.1*rng.standard_normal(block)
   elif kind==3: # mirror segment
       seg=max(20,n//20); start=rng.integers(0,n-seg); z[start:start+seg] = z[start:start+seg][::-1]; z +=0.02*rng.standard_normal(n)
   elif kind==4: # roll shift
       a2=np.roll(a, int(rng.integers(-n//100, n//100+1)))
       return normalize(a2)
   elif kind==5: # smooth low-freq
       freqs = rng.integers(1,6,size=5); amps=rng.random(5); phi = rng.uniform(0,2pi,5)
       x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       pert=0.1*sum(amp*cos(2pi f x+phi))
       z += pert
   return softmax(z - mean)
```
No lambdas. Need `np.pi`.

For roll, z not used; okay.

Now `_local_optimize` phases better:
```
def _local_optimize(a, deadline, rng=None):
    a=normalize(a); best=a; best_sc=score
    total=deadline-now
    if total<20: return best,best_sc
    # 1. LBFGS 40%
    if total>50:
       d=now+0.4*total
       a2,sc2=_lbfgs_anneal(a, d, min_remaining=20)
       if sc2<best: update
    # 2. active 15%
    if now+10<deadline:
       d=min(deadline, now+0.15*total)
       a2,sc2=_active_polish(a, d, min_remaining=5)
       update
    # 3. entropy 20%
    if now+10<deadline:
       d=min(deadline, now+0.2*total)
       a2,sc2=_entropy_hill(a, d, min_remaining=5)
       update
    # 4. ILS rest
    if now+20<deadline:
       d=deadline-10
       a2,sc2=_ils(a, d, rng)
       update
    return best,best_sc
```
Total shares may exceed deadline due to min, but time checks.

```
def _ils(a, deadline, rng=None):
   if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(1)
   current=normalize(a); current_sc=score(current)
   best=current; best_sc=current_sc
   attempt=0
   while now + 15 < deadline:
       # quick budget based on remaining, cap 15s, min 5
       rem=deadline-now
       qb=max(5,min(15, rem*0.15))
       kind=int(rng.integers(0,6))
       strength = 0.05 + 0.1*(attempt%5)
       a_p=_perturb(current,kind,rng,strength)
       # quick: active polish for qb*0.7, entropy for qb*0.3
       d1=min(deadline, now+qb*0.7)
       a_opt,sc_opt=_active_polish(a_p,d1,min_remaining=1)
       if now + max(3, qb*0.2) < deadline:
          d2=min(deadline, now+qb*0.2)
          a_opt2,sc_opt2=_entropy_hill(a_opt,d2,min_remaining=1)
          if sc_opt2<sc_opt: a_opt=a_opt2; sc_opt=sc_opt2
       if sc_opt < best_sc - 1e-12:
           best=a_opt; best_sc=sc_opt; current=a_opt.copy()
           print progress? maybe in propose, not too many prints. Could print occasionally.
       elif sc_opt < current_sc - 1e-7:
           current=a_opt.copy()
       attempt+=1
       if attempt%10==0: print ILS best
   return best,best_sc
```
This may waste if perturb worse. But active polish from worse may still good? It may not. Maybe perturb should be based on best, not current, to explore. Use current evolves.

```
def _lbfgs_anneal(a_init, deadline, min_remaining=20, start_factor=0.03):
    n=len(a_init); a=_normalize(a_init.copy()); current_a=a
    best_a=a; best_sc=_score(a)
    z=_to_z(a)
    best_z=z.copy()
    factors = [...]
    for fac in factors:
       if now+min_remain >= deadline: break
       c=_self_conv(current_a); M=float(c.max())
       if not finite or M<=0: break
       beta=max(M*fac, M*1e-12)
       if beta <= 0: break
       prob=_SmoothProblem(beta,n)
       # run until no improvement in fun or time
       current_fun=None
       consecutive_worse=0
       while now+min_remain < deadline:
          maxit=_chunk_maxit(n, deadline, min_rem)
          try: res=minimize(...)
          except: break
          if not finite res.x: break
          a_new=_softmax(res.x)
          sc_new=_score(a_new)
          if sc_new < best_sc - 1e-15:
              best_a=a_new.copy(); best_sc=sc_new; best_z=res.x.copy()
          new_fun=float(res.fun)
          if current_fun is None: current_fun=new_fun
          if new_fun < current_fun - 1e-18:
              current_a=a_new.copy(); z=res.x.copy(); current_fun=new_fun; consecutive_worse=0
          else:
              consecutive_worse +=1
              if consecutive_worse >= 3:
                  break
              # if status success break
          if res.success or res.status==0:
              break
          # if max reached continue next chunk
       # after beta, if current_a score much worse than best, reset to best
       sc_cur=_score(current_a)
       if sc_cur > best_sc + 1e-5:
           current_a=best_a.copy(); z=_to_z(best_a); best_z=z.copy()
    return best_a,best_sc
```

best=a
while time:
   c=m; M
   w from exp((c-M)/beta_w) # beta_w small
   # refine w to equalize h:
   for it in 30:
      h=_corr_hp(a,w); mean=mean(h); r=h-mean
      grad_w=_conv(a,r)
      # since minimize variance, update log_w
      log_w = log(w)
      # gradient positive where increasing w would increase variance; decreasing where high.
      update = -grad_w
      # normalize? Since w sum fixed, multiplicative and normalize.
      log_w += eta_w * update

   # compute descent direction with refined w
   h, mean, grad = 4n a(h-mean)
   line search a in direction (maybe in p-space)
   accept if score improves
   update c, w.

Let's implement `_kkt_hill`:
```
def _kkt_hill(a, deadline, min_remaining=10, rng=None):
   best=_normalize(a); best_sc=score
   z=_to_z(best)
   beta_wfac=1e-3
   step_w=0.5
   step_a=0.1
   it=0
   while now+min_rem<deadline:
      it+=1
      a=softmax(z); c=_self_conv(a); M=c.max(); sc=2nM
      if sc<best: update
      # init w
      shifted=(c-M)/(M*beta_wfac); e=...; w=...
      log_w=np.log(w)
      # refine weights 30 iterations or time
      for _ in range(30):
         if now+min_rem>=deadline: break
         h=_corr_hp(a,w)
         mean_h=float(np.mean(h))
         r=h-mean_h
         gw=_conv(a,r)
         upd=-gw
         upd-=float(np.mean(upd))
         scale=max(np.max(np.abs(upd)),1e-300)
         upd=upd/scale
         log_w = log_w + step_w*upd
         log_w = log_w - np.max(log_w)
         w=np.exp(log_w); s=w.sum(); if s<=0: w=ones; else w=w/s
      # gradient with refined w
      h=_corr_hp(a,w); mean_h=float(np.mean(h)); W=mean_h # since h mean? Wait W for gradient should be mean of h? For objective with normalized weights w, p^T F? ∑a h = dot(w,c)=W, not mean(h). But KKT gradient condition H_w a = constant; h should be constant. For descent, use grad=4n a (h - dot(w,c))? If w normalized, C_smooth = dot(w,c), gradient =4n a(h - dot(w,c)). If h approximately constant equal dot(w,c)? When h_j constant = λ, ∑a h = λ∑a^2, not λ. Wait earlier gradient formula: F_i=4n h_i, p^T F=4n dot(w,c). grad_z=4n p (h_i - dot(w,c)). If h_i constant λ, gradient not zero unless dot(w,c)=λ. But KKT uses H_w a constant =? H_w a_i =2h_i. Stationarity H_w a = λ1. The Lagrange multiplier for sum? For objective max with weights w sum1, F=2n∑w c. At optimum, ∂F/∂a_i =4n h_i = multiplier constant. Therefore h_i should be constant, and multiplier=4n λ. The gradient in simplex should be p_i F_i - p_i ∑p F? But ∑p F =4n dot(w,c). If h constant λ, ∑p F=4n dot(w,c). For gradient of constrained minimization over simplex, we should subtract the average with respect to? The gradient in z formula is correct: grad_z=4n p (h - dot(w,c)). If h constant λ, this is not zero unless dot(w,c)=λ. But because constraint sum p=1, the Lagrange multiplier is ∑p_i F_i? Wait stationarity in p with constraint sum p=1: F_i = μ for all i in interior. Thus the vector F_ i should be constant. In z parametrization, grad_z_i = p_i F_i - p_i ∑ F_j? This is zero if F_i = constant μ, because ∑ F_j p_j = μ∑p? Actually ∑_j p_j F_j = μ∑p_j=μ. Then grad=p_i μ - p_i μ=0. But our F_i =4n h_i. ∑ p_j F_j =4n ∑p h =4n dot(w,c). If h constant μ/4n, then ∑p h = μ/4n ∑p^2, not μ/4n. Contradiction: The stationarity condition is ∂F/∂p_i = ∑_j? Implementation of `_kkt_hill`:
```
def _kkt_hill(a, deadline, min_remaining=10):
    n=len(a); best=normalize(a); best_sc=score
    z=_to_z(best)
    beta_wfac=1e-3
    step_w=0.5
    step_a=0.1
    stall=0
    while now+min_rem<deadline:
       a=softmax(z); c=_self_conv(a); M=c.max(); sc=2nM
       update
       # initial w from exp peaks
       denom=max(M*beta_wfac,1e-300); shifted=(c-M)/denom; e=...; w=...
       log_w=np.log(w) - max
       # refine weights
       for _ in range(40):
          if now... break
          h=_corr_hp(a,w)
          target=float(np.dot(a,h))  # weighted
          r=h-target
          gw=_conv(a,r) # or conv(r,a)
          upd=-gw; upd-=mean(upd); scale=max(abs(upd)); if scale<1e-300 break
          log_w += step_w*(upd/scale)
          log_w-=max; w=exp; normalize
       # final gradient
       h=_corr_hp(a,w); target=float(np.dot(a,h)); grad=4n*a*(h-target)
       gmax=max(abs(grad)); if gmax<1e-18: break
       dir=-grad/gmax; dir-=mean
       # line search z with normalized dir, steps [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.02]
       best_local...
       accept, adapt step_w/step_a; if no improve stall.
```
This may be similar to active but better weights. We can use it instead of active polish in local optimize. But active polish with exp weights may be enough. KKT weight refinement cost high; use for 20% of local time.

`_make_profile` with seed. Need if kind random and rng? We can pass seed. To avoid global random, use local `np.random.default_rng(seed)` inside. That's a function call, no closure.

```
def _make_profile(n, kind="uniform", seed=12345):
    t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    if kind == 'uniform': a=np.ones(n)
    elif ...
    if kind == 'random': rng=np.random.default_rng(seed); a=rng.random(n)
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-12); return a/a.sum()
```

def _to_z(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    return np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)) 
# subtract mean in caller or function? Let function subtract mean: z.z.mean()

`_softmax` as above.

`_weights_from_c(c, beta)`:
```
def _weights_from_c(c,beta):
    m=float(c.max())
    sh=(c-m)/beta
    e=np.exp(np.clip(sh,-700,0))
    s=e.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    else: w=e/s
    return w
```

class _SmoothProblem:
    def __init__(self, beta, n):
       self.beta=beta; self.n=n
    def f(self, z): return _smooth_obj(z,self.beta,self.n)
    def g(self, z): return _smooth_grad(z,self.beta,self.n)
```

def _chunk_maxit(n, deadline, min_rem):
    rem=max(1.0, deadline - min_rem - time.monotonic())
    if rem < 5: return 20
    if n <= 1024: return 3000
    if n <= 2048: return 1500
    if n <= 4096: return 800
    if n <= 8192: return 400
    if n <= 16388: return 200
    if n <= 32768: return 100
    if n <= 65536: return 50
    return 25
```

Let's scrutinize each function for bugs.

`_conv` general:
```
def _conv(a,b):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64); b=np.asarray(b,dtype=np.float64)
    n=len(a); m=len(b); out=n+m-1
    N=_next_pow2_ge(out)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)
    return c[:out]
```
For `_self_conv`, N next_pow2_ge(2n-1). Could simply `_conv(a,a)`, but F*F more efficient? `_conv` computes rfft twice same array; inefficient. Implement separate:
```
def _self_conv(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float); n=len(a); out=2*n-1; N=_next_pow2_ge(out)
   F=np.fft.rfft(a,N); return irfft(F*F,N)[:out]
```
For entropy v = _conv(a,g), both possibly different, use `_conv`.

`_corr_hp` uses `np.fft.rfft(wr,N)` each call. If w is long, okay.

`_score` uses `_self_conv`. For candidate list final, sum maybe not1 but within. Good.

`_local_optimize`:
```
def _local_optimize(a, deadline, rng=None):
    a = _normalize(a)
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = _score(best)
    now = time.monotonic()
    if not np.isfinite(best_sc) or now + 20 >= deadline:
        return best, best_sc
    total = deadline - now
    # symmetrize initial? if improves
    sym = _normalize(a + a[::-1]); sym_sc=_score(sym)
    if sym_sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
        best=sym.copy(); best_sc=sym_sc; a=sym.copy()
    # Phase L-BFGS if total > 60
    if total > 60:
        d = min(deadline, now + 0.40 * total)
        a2, sc2 = _lbfgs_anneal(a, d, min_remaining=15)
        if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_sc - 1e-14:
            best = a2.copy(); best_sc = sc2; a = best.copy()
        else:
            # keep a from lbfgs? maybe if finite use a2 for next phases even if not best? Use best to be safe.
            a = best.copy()
    # KKT if remains
    if now + 20 < deadline:
        d = min(deadline, now + max(20, 0.25*(deadline - now)))
        a2, sc2 = _kkt_hill(a, d, min_remaining=10)
        if ... update; else a=best.copy()
    # active
    if now + 10 < deadline:
        d = min(deadline, now + max(10, 0.20*(deadline-now)))
        a2, sc2 = _active_polish(a, d, min_remaining=5, rng=rng)
        update; else a=best.copy()
    # entropy
    if now + 10 < deadline:
        d = min(deadline, now + max(10, 0.20*(deadline-now)))
        a2, sc2 = _entropy_hill(a, d, min_remaining=5, rng=rng)
        update; else a=best.copy()
    # ILS rest
    if now + 30 < deadline:
        a2, sc2 = _ils(a, deadline - 10, rng)
        update
    # final sym
    sym = _normalize(best + best[::-1]); sc=_score(sym)
    if sc<best_sc: update
    return best,best_sc
```
This may call each phase on best, not on potentially worse but promising current. Safe.

Phase budgets: If L-BFGS uses up to 40%, KKT 25% of (deadline-now) not original total; okay.

Let's write `_active_polish` fully:
```
def _active_polish(a, deadline, min_remaining=5, beta_factor_start=1e-3, beta_factor_end=1e-7, step0=0.1, rng=None):
    n=len(a)
    a=_normalize(a.copy())
    best_a=a; best_sc=_score(a)
    z=_to_z(a); best_z=z.copy()
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(0)
    step=step0
    beta_factor=beta_factor_start
    momentum=0.9
    vel=np.zeros(n)
    stall=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic()+min_remaining < deadline:
       it+=1
       a=softmax(z)
       c=_self_conv(a); M=float(c.max()); sc=2.0*n*M
       if not finite... break? 
       if sc<best_sc-1e-15: update
       denom=max(M*beta_factor, 1e-300)
       w=_weights_from_c(c,denom)
       h=_corr_hp(a,w); W=float(np.dot(w,c)); grad=4.0*n*a*(h-W)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)): break
       gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
       if gmax < 1e-18: break
       direction = -grad/gmax; direction -= float(np.mean(direction))
       vel = momentum*vel + direction
       vnorm=float(np.max(np.abs(vel)))
       if not finite or vnorm<1e-18:
           vel=direction.copy(); vnorm=float(np.max(np.abs(vel)))
       vdir=vel/vnorm
       best_local=sc; best_local_z=z; chosen_alpha=None
       multipliers=(1.0,0.5,2.0,0.25,4.0,0.125,0.05)
       for mult in multipliers:
           alpha=step*mult
           zt=z+alpha*vdir
           if not np.all(np.isfinite(zt)): continue
           at=_softmax(zt); ct=_self_conv(at); Mt=float(ct.max()); sc_t=2.0*n*Mt
           if sc_t < best_local - 1e-15:
              best_local=sc_t; best_local_z=zt.copy(); chosen_alpha=alpha
       if chosen_alpha is not None:
          z=best_local_z; a=_softmax(z); sc=best_local; update best; stall=0
          if chosen_alpha > step*1.2: step=min(0.5, step*1.15)
          elif chosen_alpha < step*0.8: step=max(1e-5, step*0.7)
          beta_factor=max(beta_factor_end, beta_factor*0.99)
       else:
          vel=np.zeros(n); stall+=1
          step=max(1e-5, step*0.7)
          beta_factor=max(beta_factor_end, beta_factor*0.7)
          if stall > 80:
             z=best_z.copy()
             z = z + 0.01*rng.standard_normal(n)
             z = z - float(np.mean(z))
             step=step0; beta_factor=1e-4; vel=np.zeros(n); stall=0
       if it % 100 ==0:
          a2=_normalize(a + a[::-1]); sc2=_score(a2)
          if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < sc - 1e-12:
             a=a2.copy(); best_a=a2.copy(); best_sc=sc2; z=_to_z(a); best_z=z.copy()
    return best_a,best_sc
```
`_weights_from_c` needs beta; if beta is denom = M*beta_factor. Good.

`_kkt_hill` similar but weight refinement. Let's write.

```
def _kkt_hill(a, deadline, min_remaining=10, rng=None):
   n=len(a); a=normalize; best...
   z=_to_z(a); best_z=z
   if rng is None: rng=...
   step=0.1; weight_steps=30; step_w=0.5; beta_wfac=1e-3
   stall=0
   while now+min_rem<deadline:
      a=softmax(z); c=self_conv; M=...; sc=...
      update
      # init w
      denom=max(M*beta_wfac,1e-300); w=_weights_from_c(c,denom)
      log_w=np.log(w) - max
      # refine weights
      for _ in range(weight_steps):
         h1=_corr_hp(a,w)
         target=float(np.dot(a,h1))
         r=h1-target
         gw=_conv(a,r)  # or _conv(r,a)
         upd=-gw
         upd-=float(np.mean(upd))
         scale=float(np.max(np.abs(upd)))
         if scale < 1e-300: break
         upd=upd/scale
         log_w=log_w+step_w*upd
         log_w=np.clip(log_w - np.max(log_w), -50.0, 0.0)
         ww=np.exp(log_w); s=ww.sum(); if s<=0: break
         w=ww/s
      h=_corr_hp(a,w); target=float(np.dot(a,h)); grad=4.0*n*a*(h-target)
      gmax=...
      dir=-grad/gmax; dir-=mean
      vel? We can no momentum, just direction. Line search steps similar.
      best_local...
      for mult in (1,0.5,2,0.25,4,0.125):
         alpha=step*mult; zt=z+alpha*dir; ...
      accept adapt step; if no improve stall, restore/perturb.
   return best
```
No momentum; maybe slower but okay. Could reuse velocity, but simple.

In weight refinement, `target = dot(a,h1)`; r weighted sum zero. `gw=_conv(a,r)`? Need B^T r for unweighted variance: `∑_j r_j a_{k-j}`. `_conv(a,r)` gives `(a*r)_k=∑_j a_j r_{k-j}`, commutative: same as ∑_j r_j a_{k-j}. Yes.

Step_w adaptation: If variance not decreasing? We can compute var before/after every 10. Simpler fixed. If no a improvement for many, reduce step_w, beta.

`step` for a line search initial 0.1.

`_entropy_hill`: Code similar but no momentum.
```
def _entropy_hill(a, deadline, min_remaining=10, rng=None):
   best...
   inner_iters=300; stall=0
   while now+min_rem<deadline:
       inner_time=min(6.0, deadline-min_rem-now)
       if inner_time<1.5: break
       g=_entropy_direction(best, max_iters=300, time_budget=inner_time*0.8, eta=0.5)
       a2,sc2=_mix_improve(best,g)
       if sc2<best_sc-1e-13: update; stall=0
       else:
           stall+=1
           if stall>4:
              # perturb and retry
              if rng is not None:
                 bestp=_perturb(best, 2, rng, 0.05)
                 g2=_entropy_direction(bestp, 200, inner_time*0.5, 0.5)
                 a3,sc3=_mix_improve(bestp,g2)
                 if sc3<best_sc-1e-13: update; stall=0
                 else: break
           # also try sym
          sym=_normalize(best+best[::-1]); scs=_score(sym); if scs<best_sc: update stall? break?
   return best
```
If no improvement, break to avoid wasting. Good.

`_entropy_direction` can be called many times; if best direction not improve, hill stops. Good.

`_mix_improve`: If g is very similar to a, skip. Compute `sim=float(np.dot(a,g))`. If sim>1-1e-6, return a,sc. But due to normalization, dot can be close.

Alphas list includes `1.0`, `0.1` etc. For extrapolation, `1.5`. Use:
```
alphas=(0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0,1.2,1.5)
for alpha in alphas:
   m=a+alpha*(g-a)
   if m.min() < -1e-8: m=np.maximum(m,0)
   m=_normalize(m)
   sc_m=_score(m)
```
If alpha negative no.

`_perturb`: Need handle kind roll. For low-freq smooth, use sum without lambda:
```
amps = rng.uniform(0.02,0.08,5)
freqs = rng.integers(1,6,5)
phases = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi,5)
x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
pert=np.zeros(n)
for i in range(5): pert += amps[i]*np.cos(2*np.pi*freqs[i]*x+phases[i])
z += 0.2*pert
```
Good.

Potential issue: `_resample` with new_n larger than 98304? We limit.

Now, let's consider if `_score` of final arr with sum1 may be slightly above evaluate due to evaluate_sequence clipping? No.

Now, let's evaluate potential performance of small search: `_local_optimize` includes ILS which for n=512 may run many perturbations in 12s; okay. But it also applies `_lbfgs_anneal` with many betas; could be too much for 12s but deadlines. Good.

In inits, add `_normalize(a_parent + rev?)`? For main length, small_init covers. Maybe add reversed parent quickly? Time. Could include in its own perturbation (mirror).

Potential bug: In `_weights_from_c`, beta could be 0 if M=0. In callers denom max. okay.

Potential bug: In `_kkt_hill`, weight refinement uses `_conv(a,r)`. If r has tiny imaginary? all real. Good. `gw` length 2n-1. update log_w. If `gw` all zero, scale tiny break.

Potential bug: `_kkt_hill` line search uses `direction` not velocity. If step too small, no progress. Adapt step. If stall >80, perturb. Good.

Potential bug: `_entropy_direction` update `log_y` with `update` from `r_centered`; if update negative large low v, log_y decreases. Clipping to [-50,0] relative. Good.

Potential bug: `_entropy_direction` if `y` sum becomes 0 due underflow? log_y max 0, exp at max 1, sum>=1. Good.

Potential bug: `_interp` with old_n maybe 0? parent size >0. small_init size >0. Good.

Now, let's compose final code. Ensure final answer only fenced python.
